Foreign policy priorities include, first of all, the restoration of its territorial integrity; elimination of the consequences of occupation of Nagorno-Karabakh and seven other regions of Azerbaijan surrounding Nagorno-Karabakh;[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-NSC_AZ-175)[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-176) integration into European and Euro-Atlantic structure; contribution to international security; cooperation with international organizations; regional cooperation and bilateral relations; strengthening of defense capability; promotion of security by domestic policy means; strengthening of democracy; preservation of ethnic and religious tolerance; scientific, educational, and cultural policy and preservation of moral values; economic and social development; enhancing internal and border security; and migration, energy, and transportation security policy.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-NSC_AZ-175)

Azerbaijan is an active member of international coalitions fighting international terrorism, and was one of the first countries to offer support after the [September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11_attacks "September 11 attacks").[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-177) The country is an active member of [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO "NATO")'s [Partnership for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace "Partnership for Peace") program, contributing to peacekeeping efforts in Kosovo, Afghanistan and Iraq.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*] Azerbaijan is also a [member](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_the_Council_of_Europe "Member states of the Council of Europe") of the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe "Council of Europe") since 2001 and maintains good relations with the European Union. The country may eventually apply for EU membership.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-NSC_AZ-175)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Ilham_Aliyev%2C_President_of_European_Council_Charles_Michel_held_one-on-one_meeting_in_Brussels_01.jpg/220px-Ilham_Aliyev%2C_President_of_European_Council_Charles_Michel_held_one-on-one_meeting_in_Brussels_01.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ilham_Aliyev,_President_of_European_Council_Charles_Michel_held_one-on-one_meeting_in_Brussels_01.jpg)

President [Ilham Aliyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilham_Aliyev "Ilham Aliyev") and President of the [European Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council "European Council") [Charles Michel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Michel "Charles Michel") in Brussels, Belgium, April 6, 2022

On 1 July 2021, the US Congress advanced legislation that will have an impact on the military aid that Washington has sent to Azerbaijan since 2012. This was because the packages to Armenia, instead, are significantly smaller.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-178)

Azerbaijan has been harshly criticized for bribing foreign officials and diplomats to promote its causes abroad and legitimize its elections at home, a practice termed [caviar diplomacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caviar_diplomacy "Caviar diplomacy").[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-179)[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-180)[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-181)[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-182) The [Azerbaijani laundromat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_laundromat "Azerbaijani laundromat") [money laundering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Money_laundering "Money laundering") operation involved the bribery of foreign politicians and journalists to serve the Azerbaijani government's public relations interests.[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-183)

### Military

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=13 "Edit section: Military")]

Main article: [Azerbaijani Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_Armed_Forces "Azerbaijani Armed Forces")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Ilham_Aliyev_and_Recep_Tayyip_Erdogan_attended_TEKNOFEST_Azerbaijan_festival_in_Baku_46.jpg/250px-Ilham_Aliyev_and_Recep_Tayyip_Erdogan_attended_TEKNOFEST_Azerbaijan_festival_in_Baku_46.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ilham_Aliyev_and_Recep_Tayyip_Erdogan_attended_TEKNOFEST_Azerbaijan_festival_in_Baku_46.jpg)

[Azerbaijani Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_Navy "Azerbaijani Navy") ships during the 2022 Teknofest Azerbaijan festival in [Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku "Baku")

The National Army of the Azerbaijan Democratic Republic was created on 26 June 1918.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-184)[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-185) When Azerbaijan gained independence after the dissolution of the Soviet Union, the Armed Forces of the Republic of Azerbaijan were created according to the law on the armed forces of 9 October 1991.[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-186) The original date of the establishment of the short-lived National Army is celebrated as Army Day (26 June).[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-187) As of 2021, Azerbaijan had 126,000 active personnel in its armed forces. There are also 17,000 paramilitary troops and 330,00 reserve personnel.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Blandy12-188) The armed forces have three branches: the [Land Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_Azerbaijan "Army of Azerbaijan"), the [Air Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_Air_Forces "Azerbaijani Air Forces") and the [Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_Navy "Azerbaijan Navy"). Additionally the armed forces embrace several military sub-groups that can be involved in state defense when needed. These are the [Internal Troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Troops_of_Azerbaijan "Internal Troops of Azerbaijan") of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and the [State Border Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Border_Service_of_Azerbaijan "State Border Service of Azerbaijan"), which includes the Coast Guard as well.[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cia-29) The [Azerbaijani National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_National_Guard "Azerbaijani National Guard") is a paramilitary force that operates as a semi-independent entity of the Special State Protection Service, an agency subordinate to the president.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-189)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Servicemen_of_the_%22Yarasa%22_Special_Forces_at_Victory_Parade_in_Baku.jpg/220px-Servicemen_of_the_%22Yarasa%22_Special_Forces_at_Victory_Parade_in_Baku.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Servicemen_of_the_%22Yarasa%22_Special_Forces_at_Victory_Parade_in_Baku.jpg)

Members of the [Special Forces of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Forces_of_Azerbaijan "Special Forces of Azerbaijan") during the [Baku Victory Parade of 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Victory_Parade_of_2020 "Baku Victory Parade of 2020")

Azerbaijan adheres to the [Treaty on Conventional Armed Forces in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_on_Conventional_Armed_Forces_in_Europe "Treaty on Conventional Armed Forces in Europe") and has signed all major international arms and weapons treaties. Azerbaijan closely cooperates with [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO "NATO") in programs such as [Partnership for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace "Partnership for Peace") and [Individual Partnership Action Plan/pfp and ipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual_Partnership_Action_Plan "Individual Partnership Action Plan"). Azerbaijan has deployed 151 of its peacekeeping forces in Iraq and another 184 in Afghanistan.[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-190)

Azerbaijan spent $2.24 billion on its defence budget as of 2020[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit),[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-191) which amounted to 5.4% of its total GDP,[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-192) and some 12.7% of general government expenditure.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-193) Azerbaijani [defense industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_Industry_of_Azerbaijan "Ministry of Defence Industry of Azerbaijan") manufactures small arms, artillery systems, tanks, armors and [night vision devices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_vision_device "Night vision device"), aviation bombs, UAVs/unmanned aerial vehicle, various military vehicles and military planes and helicopters.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-194)[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-195)[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-196)[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-197)

### Human rights and freedom

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=14 "Edit section: Human rights and freedom")]

Main article: [Human rights in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Azerbaijan "Human rights in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Rashadat_Akhundov.jpg/220px-Rashadat_Akhundov.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rashadat_Akhundov.jpg)

[Rashadat Akhundov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashadat_Akhundov "Rashadat Akhundov"), the co-founder of [Nida Civic Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nida_Civic_Movement "Nida Civic Movement"), was sentenced to eight years of imprisonment on 6 May 2014.

The constitution claims to guarantee freedom of speech, but this is denied in practice. After several years of decline in press and media freedom, in 2014, the media environment deteriorated rapidly under a governmental campaign to silence any opposition and criticism, even while the country led the Committee of Ministers of the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe "Council of Europe") (May–November 2014). Spurious legal charges and impunity in violence against journalists have remained the norm.[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-pace-198) All foreign broadcasts are banned in the country.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FH-199) According to the 2013 [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House "Freedom House") [Freedom of the Press report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_the_Press_\(report\) "Freedom of the Press (report)"), Azerbaijan's press freedom status is "not free", and Azerbaijan ranks 177th out of 196 countries.[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-200) [Radio Free Europe/Radio Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Free_Europe/Radio_Liberty "Radio Free Europe/Radio Liberty") and [Voice of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voice_of_America "Voice of America") are banned in Azerbaijan.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-201) Discrimination against [LGBT people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Azerbaijan "LGBT rights in Azerbaijan") in Azerbaijan is widespread.[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-202)[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-203)

Christianity is officially recognized. All religious communities are required to register to be allowed to meet, under the risk of imprisonment. This registration is often denied. "Racial discrimination contributes to the country's lack of religious freedom, since many of the Christians are ethnic Armenian or Russian, rather than Azeri Muslim".[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-204)[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-205)

During the last few years,\[*[when?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")*] three journalists were killed and several prosecuted in trials described as unfair by international human rights organizations. Azerbaijan had the largest number of journalists imprisoned in Europe in 2015, according to the [Committee to Protect Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists "Committee to Protect Journalists"), and is the 5th most censored country in the world, ahead of Iran and China.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-206) Some critical journalists have been [arrested](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_misinformation#Efforts_to_combat_misinformation "COVID-19 misinformation") for their coverage of the [COVID-19 pandemic in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Azerbaijan "COVID-19 pandemic in Azerbaijan").[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-207)[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-208)

A report by an [Amnesty International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amnesty_International "Amnesty International") researcher in October 2015 points to "...the severe deterioration of human rights in Azerbaijan over the past few years. Sadly Azerbaijan has been allowed to get away with unprecedented levels of repression and in the process almost wipe out its civil society."[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-209) Amnesty's 2015/16 annual report[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-210) on the country stated "... persecution of political dissent continued. Human rights organizations remained unable to resume their work. At least 18 prisoners of conscience remained in detention at the end of the year. Reprisals against independent journalists and activists persisted both in the country and abroad, while their family members also faced harassment and arrests. International human rights monitors were barred and expelled from the country. Reports of torture and other ill-treatment persisted."[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-211)

*[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian "The Guardian")* reported in April 2017 that "Azerbaijan's ruling elite operated a secret $2.9bn (£2.2bn) scheme to pay prominent Europeans, buy luxury goods and launder money through a network of opaque British companies .... Leaked data shows that the Azerbaijani leadership, accused of serial human rights abuses, systemic corruption and rigging elections, made more than 16,000 covert payments from 2012 to 2014. Some of this money went to politicians and journalists, as part of an international lobbying operation to deflect criticism of Azerbaijan's president, Ilham Aliyev, and to promote a positive image of his oil-rich country." There was no suggestion that all recipients were aware of the source of the money as it arrived via a disguised route.[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Gdn4917-212)

### Administrative divisions

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Administrative divisions")]

Main article: [Administrative divisions of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Azerbaijan "Administrative divisions of Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Economic_Regions_of_Azerbaijan_%282021%3B_labelled%29.png/400px-Economic_Regions_of_Azerbaijan_%282021%3B_labelled%29.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Economic_Regions_of_Azerbaijan_\(2021;_labelled\).png)

Azerbaijan is divided into 14 economic regions.

There are 14 economic regions; 66 [rayons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raion "Raion") (*rayonlar*, singular *rayon*) and 11 cities (*şəhərlər*, singular *şəhər*) under the direct authority of the republic.[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-213) Moreover, Azerbaijan includes the [Autonomous Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_Republic "Autonomous Republic") (*muxtar respublika*) of [Nakhchivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakhchivan_Autonomous_Republic "Nakhchivan Autonomous Republic").[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cia-29) The President of Azerbaijan appoints the governors of these units, while the government of Nakhchivan is elected and approved by the parliament of Nakhchivan Autonomous Republic.

- [Baku Economic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Economic_Region "Baku Economic Region")

## Economy

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: Economy")]

Main article: [Economy of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Azerbaijan "Economy of Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/GPD_per_capita_development_of_Azerbaijan.jpg/220px-GPD_per_capita_development_of_Azerbaijan.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:GPD_per_capita_development_of_Azerbaijan.jpg)

Change in per capita GDP of Azerbaijan since 1973. Figures are inflation-adjusted to 2011 International dollars.

After gaining independence in 1991, Azerbaijan became a member of the [International Monetary Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund "International Monetary Fund"), the [World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank "World Bank"), the [European Bank for Reconstruction and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Bank_for_Reconstruction_and_Development "European Bank for Reconstruction and Development"), the [Islamic Development Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Development_Bank "Islamic Development Bank"), and the [Asian Development Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Development_Bank "Asian Development Bank").[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZE-215) The banking system consists of the [Central Bank of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Azerbaijan "Central Bank of Azerbaijan"), [commercial banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_bank "Commercial bank"), and non-banking credit organizations. The National (now Central) Bank was created in 1992 based on the Azerbaijan State Savings Bank, an affiliate of the former State Savings Bank of the USSR. The Central Bank serves as Azerbaijan's central bank, empowered to issue the national currency, the [Azerbaijani manat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_manat "Azerbaijani manat"), and to supervise all commercial banks. Two major commercial banks are [UniBank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unibank_\(Azerbaijan\) "Unibank (Azerbaijan)") and the state-owned [International Bank of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Bank_of_Azerbaijan "International Bank of Azerbaijan"), run by Abbas Ibrahimov.[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-216)

Pushed up by spending and demand growth, the 2007 [Q1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_quarter_of_a_calendar_year "First quarter of a calendar year") inflation rate reached 16.6%.[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-INF-217) Nominal incomes and monthly wages climbed 29% and 25% respectively against this figure, but price increases in the non-oil industry encouraged inflation.[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-INF-217) Azerbaijan shows some signs of the so-called "[Dutch disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_disease "Dutch disease")" because of its fast-growing energy sector, which causes inflation and makes non-energy exports more expensive.[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-218) In the early 2000s, chronically high inflation was brought under control. This led to the launch of a new currency, the new Azerbaijani manat, on 1 January 2006, to cement the economic reforms and erase the vestiges of an unstable economy.[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-219)[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-220) Azerbaijan is also ranked 57th in the [Global Competitiveness Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Competitiveness_Report "Global Competitiveness Report") for 2010–2011, above other CIS countries.[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-221) By 2012 the GDP of Azerbaijan had increased 20-fold from its 1995 level.[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-222)

### Energy and natural resources

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=17 "Edit section: Energy and natural resources")]

Main article: [Energy in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Azerbaijan "Energy in Azerbaijan")

Further information: [Petroleum industry in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroleum_industry_in_Azerbaijan "Petroleum industry in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Oil_Rocks_near_Baku.jpg/220px-Oil_Rocks_near_Baku.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Oil_Rocks_near_Baku.jpg)

[Oil Rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_Rocks "Oil Rocks") (Neft Daşları) near Baku

Two-thirds of Azerbaijan is rich in oil and natural gas.[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZ-223) The oil industry dates back to the ancient period. Arabian historian and traveler [Ahmad Al-Baladhuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Baladhuri "Al-Baladhuri") discusses the economy of the Absheron Peninsula in antiquity, mentioning its oil in particular.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-224) There are many [pipelines in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pipelines_in_Azerbaijan "Pipelines in Azerbaijan"). The goal of the [Southern Gas Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Gas_Corridor "Southern Gas Corridor"), which connects the giant [Shah Deniz gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Deniz_gas_field "Shah Deniz gas field") in Azerbaijan to Europe,[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-naturalgas-225) is to reduce European Union's dependency on Russian gas.[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-226)

The region of the Lesser Caucasus accounts for most of the country's gold, silver, iron, copper, [titanium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanium "Titanium"), [chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium "Chromium"), [manganese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manganese "Manganese"), [cobalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cobalt "Cobalt"), [molybdenum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molybdenum "Molybdenum"), complex [ore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ore "Ore") and [antimony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antimony "Antimony").[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZ-223) In September 1994, a 30-year contract was signed between the State Oil Company of Azerbaijan Republic ([SOCAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOCAR "SOCAR")) and 13 oil companies, among them [Amoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amoco "Amoco"), [BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BP "BP"), [ExxonMobil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ExxonMobil "ExxonMobil"), [Lukoil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lukoil "Lukoil") and [Equinor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equinor "Equinor").[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZE-215) Western oil companies have been able to tap deepwater [oilfields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_field "Oil field") untouched by the Soviet exploitation. International academics consider Azerbaijan as one of the most important [oil exploration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrocarbon_exploration "Hydrocarbon exploration") and development regions.[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-227) The [State Oil Fund of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Oil_Fund_of_Azerbaijan "State Oil Fund of Azerbaijan") was established as an extra-budgetary fund to ensure [macroeconomic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macroeconomy "Macroeconomy") stability, transparency in the management of oil revenue, and safeguarding of resources for future generations.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Baku_pipelines.svg/300px-Baku_pipelines.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Baku_pipelines.svg)

The [South Caucasus Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Caucasus_Pipeline "South Caucasus Pipeline") bringing natural gas through Turkey to Europe.

Access to [biocapacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biocapacity "Biocapacity") is less than world average. In 2016, Azerbaijan had 0.8 global hectares[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-footprintdata-228) of biocapacity per person within its territory, half the world average of 1.6 global hectares per person.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-229) In 2016 Azerbaijan used 2.1 global hectares of biocapacity per person – their [ecological footprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecological_footprint "Ecological footprint") of consumption. This means they use more biocapacity than Azerbaijan contains. As a result, Azerbaijan is running a biocapacity deficit.[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-footprintdata-228)

Azeriqaz, a sub-company of SOCAR, intends to ensure full gasification of the country by 2021.[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-230) Azerbaijan was one of the sponsors of the east–west and north–south energy transport corridors. [Baku–Tbilisi–Kars railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku%E2%80%93Tbilisi%E2%80%93Kars_railway "Baku–Tbilisi–Kars railway") line connects the Caspian region with Turkey. The [Trans-Anatolian gas pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Anatolian_gas_pipeline "Trans-Anatolian gas pipeline") and [Trans Adriatic Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans_Adriatic_Pipeline "Trans Adriatic Pipeline") deliver natural gas from Azerbaijan's Shah Deniz gas to Turkey and Europe.[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-naturalgas-225) Azerbaijan extended the agreement on development of [ACG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azeri%E2%80%93Chirag%E2%80%93Gunashli "Azeri–Chirag–Gunashli") until 2050 according to the amended [PSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Production_sharing_agreement "Production sharing agreement") signed on 14 September 2017 by SOCAR and co-ventures ([BP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BP "BP"), [Chevron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chevron_Corporation "Chevron Corporation"), [Inpex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inpex "Inpex"), [Equinor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equinor "Equinor"), [ExxonMobil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ExxonMobil "ExxonMobil"), [TP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%BCrkiye_Petrolleri_Anonim_Ortakl%C4%B1%C4%9F%C4%B1 "Türkiye Petrolleri Anonim Ortaklığı"), ITOCHU and [ONGC Videsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_and_Natural_Gas_Corporation "Oil and Natural Gas Corporation")).[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-231)

### Agriculture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=18 "Edit section: Agriculture")]

Main article: [Agriculture in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Azerbaijan "Agriculture in Azerbaijan")

Azerbaijan has the largest agricultural basin in the region. About 54.9 percent of Azerbaijan is agricultural land.[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-STA-132) At the beginning of 2007 there were 4,755,100 hectares of used agricultural area.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AG-232) In the same year the total wood resources counted 136 million m3.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AG-232) Agricultural scientific research institutes are focused on meadows and pastures, horticulture and subtropical crops, green vegetables, [viticulture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viticulture "Viticulture") and [wine-making](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_wine "Azerbaijani wine"), cotton growing and [medicinal plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicinal_plants "Medicinal plants").[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-233) In some areas it is profitable to grow grain, potatoes, [sugar beets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_beet "Sugar beet"), cotton[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-234) and tobacco. Livestock, dairy products, and wine and [spirits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distilled_spirit "Distilled spirit") are also important farm products. The Caspian [fishing industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing_industry "Fishing industry") concentrates on the dwindling stocks of [sturgeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sturgeon "Sturgeon") and [beluga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beluga_\(sturgeon\) "Beluga (sturgeon)"). In 2002 the Azerbaijani [merchant marine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merchant_marine "Merchant marine") had 54 ships.

Some products previously imported from abroad have begun to be produced locally. Among them are Coca-Cola by Coca-Cola Bottlers LTD., beer by Baki-Kastel, parquet by Nehir and oil pipes by EUPEC Pipe Coating Azerbaijan.[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-235)

### Tourism

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=19 "Edit section: Tourism")]

Main article: [Tourism in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Azerbaijan "Tourism in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Shahdag01.jpg/220px-Shahdag01.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Shahdag01.jpg)

[Shahdag Mountain Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahdag_Mountain_Resort "Shahdag Mountain Resort") is the country's largest winter resort.

The country was a well-known tourist spot in the 1980s. The fall of the Soviet Union and the First Nagorno-Karabakh War during the 1990s damaged the tourist industry and the image of Azerbaijan as a tourist destination.[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-236) It was not until the 2000s that the tourism industry began to recover, and the country has since experienced a high rate of growth in the number of tourist visits and overnight stays.[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-237) In recent years, Azerbaijan has also become a popular destination for religious, spa, and [health care tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_medical_tourism "Azerbaijani medical tourism").[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Baku_Boom_Has_Yet_to_Hit_Regions-238) During winter, the [Shahdag Mountain Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahdag_Mountain_Resort "Shahdag Mountain Resort") offers skiing with state of the art facilities.[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-239)

The government has set development as an elite tourist destination as a top priority. It is a national strategy to make tourism a major, if not the single largest, contributor to the Azerbaijani economy.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-240) These activities are regulated by the [Ministry of Culture and Tourism of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_and_Tourism_\(Azerbaijan\) "Ministry of Culture and Tourism (Azerbaijan)"). There are 63 countries which have a visa-free score.[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-241) E-visa[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-242) – for a visit of foreigners of visa-required countries to the Republic of Azerbaijan. According to the Travel and Tourism Competitiveness Report 2015 of the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Economic_Forum "World Economic Forum"), Azerbaijan holds 84th place.[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-243)

According to a report by the World Travel and Tourism Council, Azerbaijan was among the top ten countries showing the strongest growth in visitor exports between 2010 and 2016,[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-244) In addition, Azerbaijan placed first (46.1%) among countries with the fastest-developing travel and tourism economies, with strong indicators for inbound international visitor spending in 2016.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-245)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Vista_de_Baku%2C_Azerbaiy%C3%A1n%2C_2016-09-26%2C_DD_101-106_PAN.jpg/800px-Vista_de_Baku%2C_Azerbaiy%C3%A1n%2C_2016-09-26%2C_DD_101-106_PAN.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Vista_de_Baku,_Azerbaiy%C3%A1n,_2016-09-26,_DD_101-106_PAN.jpg "Panoramic view of Baku, the capital and largest city of Azerbaijan")

[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Vista_de_Baku,_Azerbaiy%C3%A1n,_2016-09-26,_DD_101-106_PAN.jpg "File:Vista de Baku, Azerbaiyán, 2016-09-26, DD 101-106 PAN.jpg")

Panoramic view of [Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku "Baku"), the capital and largest city of Azerbaijan

### Transportation

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=20 "Edit section: Transportation")]

Main articles: [Transportation in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Azerbaijan "Transportation in Azerbaijan") and [Rail transport in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Azerbaijan "Rail transport in Azerbaijan")

The convenient location of Azerbaijan on the crossroad of major international traffic arteries, such as the [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road "Silk Road") and the south–north corridor, highlights the strategic importance of the transportation sector for the country's economy.[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-246) The transport sector includes roads, railways, aviation, and maritime transport. It is also an important economic hub in the transportation of raw materials. The [Baku–Tbilisi–Ceyhan pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku%E2%80%93Tbilisi%E2%80%93Ceyhan_pipeline "Baku–Tbilisi–Ceyhan pipeline") (BTC) became operational in May 2006 and extends more than 1,774 km (1,102 mi) through the territories of Azerbaijan, Georgia, and Turkey. The BTC is designed to transport up to 50 million tons of crude oil annually and carries oil from the Caspian Sea oilfields to global markets.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-247) The [South Caucasus Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Caucasus_Pipeline "South Caucasus Pipeline"), also stretching through the territory of Azerbaijan, Georgia, and Turkey, became operational at the end of 2006 and offers additional gas supplies to the European market from the [Shah Deniz gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Deniz_gas_field "Shah Deniz gas field"). Shah Deniz is expected to produce up to 296 billion cubic meters of natural gas per year.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-248) Azerbaijan also plays a major role in the EU-sponsored Silk Road Project.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-249)

In 2002, the government established the Ministry of Transport with a broad range of policy and regulatory functions. In the same year, the country became a member of the [Vienna Convention on Road Traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna_Convention_on_Road_Traffic "Vienna Convention on Road Traffic").[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-250) Priorities are upgrading the transport network and improving transportation services to better facilitate the development of other sectors of the economy.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*] The 2012 construction of Kars–Tbilisi–Baku railway was meant to improve transportation between Asia and Europe by connecting the railways of China and Kazakhstan in the east to the European railway system in the west via Turkey. In 2010 [Broad-gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_gauge "Russian gauge") railways and electrified railways stretched for 2,918 km (1,813 mi) and 1,278 km (794 mi) respectively. By 2010, there were 35 airports and one [heliport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heliport "Heliport").[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cia-29)

### Science and technology

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=21 "Edit section: Science and technology")]

Main articles: [Science and technology in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Azerbaijan "Science and technology in Azerbaijan"), [Communications in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications_in_Azerbaijan "Communications in Azerbaijan"), [Azerbaijan National Aerospace Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_National_Aerospace_Agency "Azerbaijan National Aerospace Agency"), and [List of Azerbaijani inventions and discoveries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Azerbaijani_inventions_and_discoveries "List of Azerbaijani inventions and discoveries")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Shamakhi_observatory.jpg/220px-Shamakhi_observatory.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Shamakhi_observatory.jpg)

[Shamakhi Astrophysical Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamakhi_Astrophysical_Observatory "Shamakhi Astrophysical Observatory")

In the 21st century, a new oil and gas boom helped improve the situation in the science and technology sectors. The government launched a campaign aimed at modernization and innovation. The government estimates that profits from the information technology and communication industry will grow and become comparable to those from oil production.[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-251) Azerbaijan has a large and steadily growing Internet sector. In 2012, rapid growth was forecast for at least five more years.[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-252) Azerbaijan was ranked 95th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index "Global Innovation Index") in 2024.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-253)

The country has been making progress in developing its telecoms sector. The Ministry of Communications & Information Technologies and an operator through its role in Aztelekom are both policy-makers and regulators. Public payphones are available for local calls and require the purchase of a token from the telephone exchange or some shops and kiosks. Tokens allow a call of indefinite duration. As of 2009[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit), there were 1,397,000 main telephone lines[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-254) and 1,485,000 internet users.[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-255) There are four [GSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GSM "GSM") providers: [Azercell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azercell "Azercell"), [Bakcell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakcell "Bakcell"), Azerfon ([Nar Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nar_Mobile "Nar Mobile")), [Nakhtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakhtel "Nakhtel") mobile network operators and one [CDMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CDMA "CDMA").

In the 21st century several prominent Azerbaijani [geodynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geodynamics "Geodynamics") and [geotectonics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geotectonics "Geotectonics") scientists, inspired by the fundamental works of Elchin Khalilov and others, designed hundreds of earthquake prediction stations and earthquake-resistant buildings that now constitute the bulk of The Republican Center of Seismic Service.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-256)[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-257)[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-258) The [Azerbaijan National Aerospace Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_National_Aerospace_Agency "Azerbaijan National Aerospace Agency") launched its first satellite [AzerSat 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AzerSat_1 "AzerSat 1") into orbit on 7 February 2013 from [Guiana Space Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guiana_Space_Centre "Guiana Space Centre") in French Guiana at orbital positions 46° East.[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-259)[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-260)[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-261) The satellite covers Europe and a significant part of Asia and Africa and serves the transmission of TV and radio broadcasting as well as the Internet.[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-262) The launching of a satellite into orbit is Azerbaijan's first step in realizing its goal of becoming a nation with its own space industry, capable of successfully implementing more projects in the future.[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-263)[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-264)

## Demographics

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=22 "Edit section: Demographics")]

Main articles: [Demographics of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Azerbaijan "Demographics of Azerbaijan") and [List of cities in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Azerbaijan "List of cities in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Azerbaijan_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png/220px-Azerbaijan_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Azerbaijan_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png)

Population pyramid

As of March 2022, 52.9% of the population of 10,164,464 is urban, with the remaining 47.1% being rural.[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-reportpop-265) In January 2019, the 50.1% of the total population was female. The [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio "Human sex ratio") in the same year was 0.99 males per female.[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-266) The 2011 population growth-rate was 0.85%, compared to 1.09% worldwide.[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cia-29) A significant factor restricting population growth is a high level of migration. In 2011 Azerbaijan saw a migration of −1.14/1,000 people.[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cia-29)

The [Azerbaijani diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_diaspora "Azerbaijani diaspora") is found in 42 countries[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-267) and in turn there are many centers for ethnic minorities inside Azerbaijan, including the [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germans_in_Azerbaijan "Germans in Azerbaijan") cultural society "Karelhaus", [Slavic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavic_peoples "Slavic peoples") cultural center, Azerbaijani-Israeli community, [Kurdish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_people "Kurdish people") cultural center, International [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_people "Talysh people") Association, [Lezgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgins "Lezgins") national center "Samur", Azerbaijani-[Tatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatarstan "Tatarstan") community, [Crimean Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimean_Tatars "Crimean Tatars") society, etc.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-MIN-268)

In total, Azerbaijan has 78 cities, 63 city districts, and one special legal status city. 261 urban-type settlements and 4248 villages follow these.[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-stat.gov.az-269)

### Ethnicity

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=23 "Edit section: Ethnicity")]

Main articles: [Azerbaijanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijanis "Azerbaijanis") and [Ethnic minorities in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_minorities_in_Azerbaijan "Ethnic minorities in Azerbaijan")

Demographics of Azerbaijan

  [Azerbaijanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijanis "Azerbaijanis") (91.6%)

  [Lezgins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgins "Lezgins") (2.02%)

  [Russians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians "Russians") (1.35%)

  [Armenians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenians "Armenians") (1.35%)

  [Talyshs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talyshs "Talyshs") (1.26%)

  [Avars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avars_\(Caucasus\) "Avars (Caucasus)") (0.56%)

  [Turks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_people "Turkish people") (0.43%)

  [Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars "Tatars") (0.29%)

  [Tats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tat_people_\(Caucasus\) "Tat people (Caucasus)") (0.28%)

  [Ukrainians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainians "Ukrainians") (0.24%)

  [Tsakhurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsakhurs "Tsakhurs") (0.14%)

  [Udis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udis "Udis") (0.04%)

  [Georgians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgians "Georgians") (0.11%)

  Jews (0.1%)

  [Kurds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurds "Kurds") (0.07%)

  Others (0.16%)

The ethnic composition of the population according to the 2009 population census: 91.6% [Azerbaijanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_people "Azerbaijani people"), 2.0% [Lezgins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgians "Lezgians"), 1.4% [Armenians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenians "Armenians") (almost all Armenians live in the break-away region of [Nagorno-Karabakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Nagorno-Karabakh "Republic of Nagorno-Karabakh")), 1.3% [Russians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russians "Russians"), 1.3% [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_people "Talysh people"), 0.6% [Avars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caucasian_Avars "Caucasian Avars"), 0.4% [Turkish people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_people "Turkish people"), 0.3% [Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars "Tatars"), 0.3% [Tats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tat_people_\(Caucasus\) "Tat people (Caucasus)"), 0.2% [Ukrainians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainians "Ukrainians"), 0.1% [Tsakhurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsakhur_people "Tsakhur people"), 0.1% [Georgians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgians "Georgians"), 0.1% Jews, 0.1% [Kurds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_people "Kurdish people"), other 0.2%.[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-auto-270)

### Languages

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=24 "Edit section: Languages")]

Main article: [Languages of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Azerbaijan "Languages of Azerbaijan")

The official language is [Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language "Azerbaijani language"), a [Turkic language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkic_languages "Turkic languages"). Approximately 92% of the national population speak it as their [mother tongue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_tongue "Mother tongue").[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-UNLANGDATA-271) Russian and [Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_language "Armenian language") (only in Nagorno-Karabakh) are still spoken in Azerbaijan. Each is the mother tongue of around 1.5% of the national population.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-UNLANGDATA-271) In 1989, Armenian was the majority language in the region of Nagorno-Karabakh, spoken by about 76% of the regional population.[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-272) After the first Nagorno-Karabakh war, native speakers of Armenian composed around 95% of the regional population.[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-273)

A dozen other minority languages are spoken natively,[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-274) including [Avar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avar_language "Avar language"), [Budukh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budukh_language "Budukh language"),[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) [Georgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_language "Georgian language"), [Juhuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juhuri_language "Juhuri language"),[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) [Khinalug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khinalug_language "Khinalug language"),[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) [Kryts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryts_language "Kryts language"),[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) [Lezgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgian_language "Lezgian language"), [Rutul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutul_language "Rutul language"),[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_language "Talysh language"), [Tat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tat_language_\(Caucasus\) "Tat language (Caucasus)"),[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) [Tsakhur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsakhur_language "Tsakhur language"),[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) and [Udi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udi_language "Udi language").[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Encyclopedia_of_the_World's_Endangered_Languages-275) All these are spoken only by small minority populations, some of which are tiny and decreasing.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-276)

### Religion

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=25 "Edit section: Religion")]

Main articles: [Religion in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Azerbaijan "Religion in Azerbaijan") and [Irreligion in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_Azerbaijan "Irreligion in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Bibi_Heybat_Mosque_Baku_1.jpg/220px-Bibi_Heybat_Mosque_Baku_1.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bibi_Heybat_Mosque_Baku_1.jpg)

The [Bibi-Heybat Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibi-Heybat_Mosque "Bibi-Heybat Mosque") in Baku. The mosque is built over the tomb of a descendant of [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad "Muhammad")[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Bibi-277)

Azerbaijan is considered the most secular Muslim-majority country.[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-278) Around 97% of the population are Muslims.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-279) Around 55–65% of Muslims are estimated to be [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_Islam "Shia Islam"), while 35–45% of Muslims are [Sunnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Muslim "Sunni Muslim").[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Pres-280)[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-281)[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-282)[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-283) Other faiths are practised by the country's various ethnic groups. Under article 48 of its constitution, Azerbaijan is a [secular state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_state "Secular state") and ensures religious freedom. In a 2006–2008 [Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_\(company\) "Gallup (company)") poll, only 21% of respondents from Azerbaijan stated that religion is an important part of their daily lives.[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-284)

Of the nation's religious minorities, the estimated 280,000 Christians (3.1%)[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-285) are mostly [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church "Russian Orthodox Church") and [Georgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_Orthodox_Church "Georgian Orthodox Church") [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church "Eastern Orthodox Church") and [Armenian Apostolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church "Armenian Apostolic Church") (almost all Armenians live in the break-away region of Nagorno-Karabakh).[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cia-29) In 2003, there were 250 [Roman Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholics "Roman Catholics").[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-286) Other Christian denominations as of 2002 include [Lutherans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheranism "Lutheranism"), [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists "Baptists") and [Molokans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molokan "Molokan").[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZR-287) There is also a small [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant "Protestant") community.[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-288)[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-289) Azerbaijan also has an ancient Jewish population with a [2,000-year history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Jews "Mountain Jews"); Jewish organizations\[*[who?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Words_to_watch#Unsupported_attributions "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Words to watch")*] estimate that 12,000 Jews remain in Azerbaijan, which is home to the [only Jewish-majority town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Q%C4%B1rm%C4%B1z%C4%B1_Q%C9%99s%C9%99b%C9%99 "Qırmızı Qəsəbə") outside of Israel and the United States.[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-290)[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-291)[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-292)[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-293) Azerbaijan also is home to members of the [Baháʼí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith "Baháʼí Faith"), [Hare Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Society_for_Krishna_Consciousness "International Society for Krishna Consciousness") and [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses "Jehovah's Witnesses") communities, as well as adherents of the other religious communities.[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZR-287) Some religious communities have been [unofficially restricted from religious freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_Azerbaijan#Restrictions_on_religious_freedom "Freedom of religion in Azerbaijan"). A [U.S. State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._State_Department "U.S. State Department") report on the matter mentions detention of members of certain Muslim and Christian groups, and many groups have difficulty registering with the agency who regulates religion, [The State Committee on Religious Associations of the Republic of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Committee_on_Affairs_with_Religious_Associations_of_the_Republic_of_Azerbaijan "State Committee on Affairs with Religious Associations of the Republic of Azerbaijan").[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-294)

### Education

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=26 "Edit section: Education")]

Main article: [Education in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Azerbaijan "Education in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/Dunya1.jpg/220px-Dunya1.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Dunya1.jpg)

Classroom in [Dunya School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khazar_University "Khazar University")

A relatively high percentage of Azerbaijanis have obtained some form of higher education, most notably in scientific and technical subjects.[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZ_study-295) In the Soviet era, literacy and average education levels rose dramatically from their very low starting point, despite two changes in the standard alphabet, from [Perso-Arabic script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perso-Arabic_script "Perso-Arabic script") to [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_script "Latin script") in the 1920s and from Roman to [Cyrillic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrillic "Cyrillic") in the 1930s. According to Soviet data, 100 percent of males and females (ages nine to forty-nine) were literate in 1970.[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-AZ_study-295) According to the [United Nations Development Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Program "United Nations Development Program") Report 2009, the literacy rate is 99.5 percent.[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-296)

Since independence, one of the first laws that Parliament passed to disassociate itself from the Soviet Union was to adopt a [modified-Latin alphabet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_alphabet "Azerbaijani alphabet") to replace Cyrillic.[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-297) Other than that the Azerbaijani system has undergone little structural change. Initial alterations have included the reestablishment of religious education (banned during the Soviet period) and curriculum changes that have reemphasized the use of the Azerbaijani language and have eliminated ideological content. In addition to elementary schools, the education institutions include thousands of preschools, general secondary schools, and [vocational schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_schools "Vocational schools"), including specialized secondary schools and technical schools. Education through the ninth grade is compulsory.[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:02-298)

## Culture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=27 "Edit section: Culture")]

The [Azerbaijani carpet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_rug "Azerbaijani rug") and [Kalaghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalaghai "Kalaghai"), a [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [Masterpiece of Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity "Masterpieces of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity")

[![An Azerbaijani carpet from the Shirvan group from Bijo village, mid-19th century](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/%C5%9E%C9%99kil-Bico_xal%C3%A7as%C4%B1_%281%29.jpg/210px-%C5%9E%C9%99kil-Bico_xal%C3%A7as%C4%B1_%281%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:%C5%9E%C9%99kil-Bico_xal%C3%A7as%C4%B1_\(1\).jpg)

[![Silk Kalaghais](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Az%C9%99rbaycan_ip%C9%99yind%C9%99n_k%C9%99la%C4%9Fay%C4%B1lar_1.JPG/200px-Az%C9%99rbaycan_ip%C9%99yind%C9%99n_k%C9%99la%C4%9Fay%C4%B1lar_1.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Az%C9%99rbaycan_ip%C9%99yind%C9%99n_k%C9%99la%C4%9Fay%C4%B1lar_1.JPG)

The [culture of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Azerbaijan "Culture of Azerbaijan") has developed as a result of many influences; that is why Azerbaijanis are, in many ways, bi-cultural. National traditions are preserved despite Western influences, including globalized consumer culture. For example, [Novruz Bayram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novruz_in_Azerbaijan "Novruz in Azerbaijan") is a family holiday derived from the traditional celebration of the New Year in [Zoroastrianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism "Zoroastrianism").[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-299)

Azerbaijani national and traditional dresses are the [chokha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chokha "Chokha") and [papakhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papakhi "Papakhi"). There are radio broadcasts in Russian, [Georgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgian_language "Georgian language"), [Kurdish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_language "Kurdish language"), [Lezgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lezgian_language "Lezgian language") and [Talysh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talysh_language "Talysh language") languages, which are financed from the state budget.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-MIN-268) Some local radio stations in [Balakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balak%C9%99n "Balakən") and [Khachmaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khachmaz_\(city\) "Khachmaz (city)") organize broadcasts in [Avar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avar_language "Avar language") and [Tat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tat_language_\(Caucasus\) "Tat language (Caucasus)").[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-MIN-268) In Baku several newspapers are published in Russian, Kurdish (*Dengi Kurd*), Lezgian (*Samur*) and Talysh languages.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-MIN-268) Jewish society "Sokhnut" publishes the newspaper *Aziz*.[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-MIN-268)

### Architecture

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=28 "Edit section: Architecture")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Baku_White_City_under_construction.jpg/220px-Baku_White_City_under_construction.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Baku_White_City_under_construction.jpg)

[Baku White City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_White_City "Baku White City") in [Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku "Baku"), was opened in 2011 and completed in 2022

[Azerbaijani architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Azerbaijan "Architecture of Azerbaijan") typically combines elements of [East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_world "Eastern world") and [West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_culture "Western culture"),[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-300) with heavy influences from [Persian architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_architecture "Iranian architecture"). Many ancient architectural treasures are preserved, such as the [Maiden Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maiden_Tower_\(Baku\) "Maiden Tower (Baku)") and [Palace of the Shirvanshahs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_the_Shirvanshahs "Palace of the Shirvanshahs") in the Walled City of Baku. Entries on the [UNESCO World Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage "UNESCO World Heritage") tentative list include the [Ateshgah of Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ateshgah_of_Baku "Ateshgah of Baku"), [Momine Khatun Mausoleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Momine_Khatun_Mausoleum "Momine Khatun Mausoleum"), [Hirkan National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirkan_National_Park "Hirkan National Park"), [Binagadi asphalt lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binagadi_asphalt_lake "Binagadi asphalt lake"), [Lökbatan Mud Volcano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%B6kbatan_Mud_Volcano "Lökbatan Mud Volcano"), [Shusha State Historical and Architectural Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shusha_State_Historical_and_Architectural_Reserve "Shusha State Historical and Architectural Reserve"), Baku Stage Mountain, Caspian Shore Defensive Constructions, Ordubad National Reserve and the [Palace of Shaki Khans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Shaki_Khans "Palace of Shaki Khans").[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-301)[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-302)

Among other architectural treasures are [Quadrangular Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrangular_castle_\(Mardakan\) "Quadrangular castle (Mardakan)") in [Mardakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mardakan "Mardakan"), [Parigala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parigala "Parigala") in [Yukhary Chardaglar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuxar%C4%B1_%C3%87ardaqlar "Yuxarı Çardaqlar"), several bridges spanning the Aras River, and several mausoleums. In the 19th and early 20th centuries, little monumental architecture was created, but distinctive residences were built in Baku and elsewhere. Among the most recent architectural monuments, the [Baku subways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Metro "Baku Metro") are noted for their lavish decor.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-303)

The task for modern Azerbaijani architecture is diverse application of modern aesthetics, the search for an architect's own artistic style and inclusion of the existing historico-cultural environment. Major projects such as [Heydar Aliyev Cultural Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heydar_Aliyev_Cultural_Center "Heydar Aliyev Cultural Center"), [Flame Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flame_Towers "Flame Towers"), [Baku Crystal Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Crystal_Hall "Baku Crystal Hall"), [Baku White City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_White_City "Baku White City") and [SOCAR Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOCAR_Tower "SOCAR Tower") have transformed the country's skyline and promotes its contemporary identity.[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-304)[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-305)

### Music and dance

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=29 "Edit section: Music and dance")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Uzeyir_Hajibeyov.jpg/170px-Uzeyir_Hajibeyov.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Uzeyir_Hajibeyov.jpg)

[Uzeyir Hajibeyov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzeyir_Hajibeyov "Uzeyir Hajibeyov") merged traditional [Azerbaijani music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_music "Azerbaijani music") with Western styles in the early 20th century.

[Music of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Azerbaijan "Music of Azerbaijan") builds on [folk traditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music "Folk music") that reach back nearly a thousand years,[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-306) evolving around the badge of [monody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monody "Monody"), producing rhythmically diverse melodies.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-EMD-307) The music has a branchy [mode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_mode "Musical mode") system, where [chromatization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromaticism "Chromaticism") of [major and minor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_and_minor "Major and minor") [scales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musical_scale "Musical scale") is of great importance.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-EMD-307) Among national musical instruments there are 14 [string instruments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_instrument "String instrument"), eight percussion instruments and six wind instruments.[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-308) According to *[The Grove Dictionary of Music and Musicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Grove_Dictionary_of_Music_and_Musicians "The New Grove Dictionary of Music and Musicians")*, "in terms of ethnicity, culture and religion the Azerbaijani are musically much closer to Iran than Turkey."[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-309)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Pht-Vugar_Ibadov_eurovision_%282%29.jpg/220px-Pht-Vugar_Ibadov_eurovision_%282%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Pht-Vugar_Ibadov_eurovision_\(2\).jpg)

[Alim Qasimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alim_Qasimov "Alim Qasimov") performs [mugham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugham "Mugham") at [Eurovision Song Contest 2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2012 "Eurovision Song Contest 2012"). The Azerbaijani Mugham was inscribed in 2008 as a [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [Masterpiece of Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity "Masterpieces of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity").

[Mugham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugham "Mugham") is usually a suite with poetry and instrumental interludes. When performing mugham, the singers have to transform their emotions into singing and music. In contrast to the mugham traditions of Central Asian countries, Azerbaijani mugham is more free-form and less rigid; it is often compared to the improvised field of [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz "Jazz").[\[307\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-310) [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") proclaimed the Azerbaijani mugham tradition a [Masterpiece of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity "Masterpieces of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity"). [Meykhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meykhana "Meykhana") is a kind of traditional Azerbaijani distinctive folk unaccompanied song, usually performed by several people improvising on a particular subject.[\[308\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-311)

[Ashiq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashik "Ashik") combines poetry, storytelling, dance, and vocal and instrumental music into a traditional performance art that stands as a symbol of Azerbaijani culture. It is a mystic [troubadour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troubadour "Troubadour") or traveling bard who sings and plays the [saz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baglama "Baglama"). This tradition has its origin in the [shamanistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamanism "Shamanism") beliefs of ancient [Turkic peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkic_peoples "Turkic peoples").[\[309\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-European_University_Institute,_Florence,_Italy-312) Ashiqs' songs are semi-improvised around common bases. Azerbaijan's ashiq art was included in the list of [Intangible Cultural Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intangible_Cultural_Heritage "Intangible Cultural Heritage") by UNESCO in 2009.[\[310\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-313)

Since the mid-1960s, Western-influenced [Azerbaijani pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_pop_music "Azerbaijani pop music"), in its various forms, that has been growing in popularity in Azerbaijan, while genres such as [rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_rock "Azerbaijani rock") and [hip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_hip_hop "Azerbaijani hip hop") are widely produced and enjoyed. Azerbaijani pop and [Azerbaijani folk music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_folk_music "Azerbaijani folk music") arose with the international popularity of performers like [Alim Qasimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alim_Qasimov "Alim Qasimov"), [Rashid Behbudov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashid_Behbudov "Rashid Behbudov"), [Vagif Mustafazadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vagif_Mustafazadeh "Vagif Mustafazadeh"), [Muslim Magomayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Magomayev_\(musician\) "Muslim Magomayev (musician)"), [Shovkat Alakbarova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shovkat_Alakbarova "Shovkat Alakbarova") and [Rubaba Muradova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubaba_Muradova "Rubaba Muradova").[\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-314) Azerbaijan is an enthusiastic participant in the Eurovision Song Contest. Azerbaijan made its debut appearance at the 2008 [Eurovision Song Contest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2008 "Eurovision Song Contest 2008"). The [country's entry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest_2009 "Azerbaijan in the Eurovision Song Contest 2009") gained third place in 2009 and fifth the following year.[\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-315) [Ell and Nikki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eldar_%26_Nigar "Eldar & Nigar") won the first place at the [Eurovision Song Contest 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2011 "Eurovision Song Contest 2011") with the song "[Running Scared](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Running_Scared_\(Ell_%26_Nikki_song\) "Running Scared (Ell & Nikki song)")", entitling Azerbaijan to host the contest in [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2012 "Eurovision Song Contest 2012"), in Baku.[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-316)[\[314\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-317) They have qualified for every Grand Final up until the [2018 edition of the contest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2018 "Eurovision Song Contest 2018"), entering with [X My Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_My_Heart_\(song\) "X My Heart (song)") by singer [Aisel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aisel_\(singer\) "Aisel (singer)").[\[315\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-318)

There are dozens of [Azerbaijani folk dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_dances "Azerbaijani dances"). They are performed at formal celebrations and the dancers wear national clothes like the chokha, which is well-preserved within the national dances. Most dances have a very fast rhythm.[\[316\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-319)

### Art

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=30 "Edit section: Art")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Azerbaijan_tradition.jpg/170px-Azerbaijan_tradition.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Azerbaijan_tradition.jpg)

Traditional Azerbaijani clothing and musical instruments

[Azerbaijani art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_art "Azerbaijani art") is represented by a wide range of handicrafts, such as [chasing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Repouss%C3%A9_and_chasing "Repoussé and chasing"), jeweling, engraving in metal, carving in wood, stone, or bone, carpet-making, lasing, pattern weaving and printing, and knitting and embroidery. Each of these types of decorative art, evidence of the endowments of the Azerbaijan nation, is very much in favor here. Many interesting facts pertaining to the development of arts and crafts in Azerbaijan were reported by numerous merchants, travelers, and diplomats who had visited these places at different times.[\[317\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-320)

The [Azerbaijani carpet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_rug "Azerbaijani rug") is a traditional handmade textile of various sizes, with a dense texture and a pile or pile-less surface, whose patterns are characteristic of Azerbaijan's many carpet-making regions. In November 2010 the Azerbaijani carpet was proclaimed a [Masterpiece of Intangible Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity "Masterpieces of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity") by UNESCO.[\[318\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-321)[\[319\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-322) Azerbaijani carpets can be categorized under several large groups and a multitude of subgroups. Scientific research of the Azerbaijani carpet is connected with the name of [Latif Karimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latif_Karimov "Latif Karimov"), a prominent Soviet-era scientist and artist.[\[320\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-323)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Lahic_folk_art.png/220px-Lahic_folk_art.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lahic_folk_art.png)

Handwork coppery in [Lahij](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lah%C4%B1c,_Ismailli "Lahıc, Ismailli")

Azerbaijan has been since ancient times known as a center of a large variety of crafts. Archeology testifies to the well-developed agriculture, stock raising, metalworking, pottery, ceramics, and carpet-weaving that date as far back as to the 2nd millennium BC. Archeological sites in Dashbulaq, Hasansu, Zayamchai, and Tovuzchai uncovered from the BTC pipeline have revealed early Iron Age artifacts.[\[321\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-324)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Sheki_dvoretc_rospisi_az.jpg/400px-Sheki_dvoretc_rospisi_az.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sheki_dvoretc_rospisi_az.jpg)

A [miniature painting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miniature_\(illuminated_manuscript\) "Miniature (illuminated manuscript)") of a battle scene on the walls of the [Palace of Shaki Khans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Shaki_Khans "Palace of Shaki Khans"), 18th century, [city of Shaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaki,_Azerbaijan "Shaki, Azerbaijan")

The [Gamigaya Petroglyphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamigaya_Petroglyphs "Gamigaya Petroglyphs"), which date back to the 1st to 4th millennium BC, are located in Azerbaijan's [Ordubad District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordubad_District "Ordubad District"). They consist of some 1,500 dislodged and carved rock paintings with images of deer, goats, bulls, dogs, snakes, birds, fantastic beings, and people, carriages, and various symbols were found on basalt rocks.[\[322\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-325) Norwegian [ethnographer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnographer "Ethnographer") and adventurer [Thor Heyerdahl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thor_Heyerdahl "Thor Heyerdahl") was convinced that people from the area went to [Scandinavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scandinavia "Scandinavia") in about 100 AD, took their boatbuilding skills with them, and transmuted them into the [Viking boats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longship "Longship") in northern Europe.[\[323\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-326)[\[324\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-327)

Over the centuries, Azerbaijani art has gone through many stylistic changes. Painting is traditionally characterized by a warmth of colour and light, as exemplified in the works of [Azim Azimzade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azim_Azimzade "Azim Azimzade") and [Bahruz Kangarli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahruz_Kangarli "Bahruz Kangarli"), and a preoccupation with religious figures and cultural motifs.[\[325\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-328) Azerbaijani painting enjoyed preeminence in Caucasus for hundreds of years, from the [Romanesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_art "Romanesque art") and [Ottoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_Ottoman_Empire "Culture of the Ottoman Empire") periods, and through the [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_realism "Socialist realism") and [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque "Baroque") periods, the latter two of which saw fruition in Azerbaijan. Notable artists include [Sattar Bahlulzade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sattar_Bahlulzade "Sattar Bahlulzade"), [Togrul Narimanbekov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Togrul_Narimanbekov "Togrul Narimanbekov"), [Tahir Salahov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahir_Salahov "Tahir Salahov"), [Alakbar Rezaguliyev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alakbar_Rezaguliyev "Alakbar Rezaguliyev"), [Mirza Gadim Iravani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirza_Gadim_Iravani "Mirza Gadim Iravani"), [Mikayil Abdullayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikayil_Abdullayev "Mikayil Abdullayev") and [Boyukagha Mirzazade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boyukagha_Mirzazade "Boyukagha Mirzazade").[\[326\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-329)
