### Literature

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=31 "Edit section: Literature")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Khurshidbanu_Natavan.jpg/180px-Khurshidbanu_Natavan.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Khurshidbanu_Natavan.jpg)

Painting of [Khurshidbanu Natavan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khurshidbanu_Natavan "Khurshidbanu Natavan"), one of the most distinguished Azerbaijani poets. She was also the daughter of the last ruler of the [Karabakh Khanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karabakh_Khanate "Karabakh Khanate").

The earliest known figure in written [Azerbaijani literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_literature "Azerbaijani literature") was [Izzeddin Hasanoghlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izzeddin_Hasanoghlu "Izzeddin Hasanoghlu"), who composed a [divan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diwan_\(poetry\) "Diwan (poetry)") consisting of Persian and Azerbaijani [ghazals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghazal "Ghazal").[\[327\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-beale-330)[\[328\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-caferoglu-331) In Persian ghazals he used a pen-name, while his Azerbaijani ghazals were composed under his own name of Hasanoghlu.[\[327\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-beale-330) Among the medieval authors was Persian poet and philosopher [Nizami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizami_Ganjavi "Nizami Ganjavi"), called Ganjavi after his place of birth, [Ganja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ganja,_Azerbaijan "Ganja, Azerbaijan"), who was the author of the [Khamsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamsa_of_Nizami "Khamsa of Nizami") ("The Quintuplet"), composed of five romantic poems, including "The Treasure of Mysteries", "Khosrow and Shīrīn", and "Leyli and Mejnūn".[\[329\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-332)

Classical literature was formed in the 14th century based on the various Early Middle Ages dialects of [Tabriz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabriz "Tabriz") and [Shirvan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirvan "Shirvan"). Among the poets of this period were [Gazi Burhanaddin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadi_Burhan_al-Din "Kadi Burhan al-Din"), Haqiqi (pen-name of [Jahan Shah Qara Qoyunlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jahan_Shah "Jahan Shah")), and [Habibi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habibi_\(poet\) "Habibi (poet)").[\[330\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-tyrrell1-333) The end of the 14th century was the start of literary activity of [Imadaddin Nasimi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nesimi "Nesimi"),[\[331\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-334) one of the greatest Azerbaijani[\[332\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-335)[\[333\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-burrill-336)[\[334\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-337) [Hurufi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurufism "Hurufism") mystical poets of the late 14th and early 15th centuries[\[335\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-nesimibritannica-338) and one of the most prominent early divan masters in Turkic literary history,[\[335\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-nesimibritannica-338) who also composed poetry in Persian[\[333\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-burrill-336)[\[336\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-babinger-339) and Arabic.[\[335\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-nesimibritannica-338) The divan and ghazal styles were further developed by poets [Qasem-e Anvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qasem-e_Anvar "Qasem-e Anvar"), [Fuzuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuzuli_\(poet\) "Fuzuli (poet)") and [Safavid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safavid_dynasty "Safavid dynasty") [Shah Ismail I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Ismail_I "Shah Ismail I") who wrote under the pen name "Khata'i".

The *[Book of Dede Korkut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Dede_Korkut "Book of Dede Korkut")* consists of two manuscripts copied in the 16th century,[\[337\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Meeker-340) and was not written earlier than the 15th century.[\[338\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Kafadar-341)[\[339\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-IranicaOguzKhan-342) It is a collection of 12 stories reflecting the oral tradition of Oghuz nomads.[\[339\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-IranicaOguzKhan-342) The 16th-century poet [Fuzuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuzuli_\(poet\) "Fuzuli (poet)") produced his timeless philosophical and lyrical *Qazals* in Arabic, Persian, and Azerbaijani. Benefiting immensely from the fine literary traditions of his environment, and building upon the legacy of his predecessors, Fuzuli was destined to become the leading literary figure of his society. His major works include *The Divan of Ghazals* and *The Qasidas*. In the same century, Azerbaijani literature further flourished with the development of ashik ([Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language "Azerbaijani language"): *Aşıq*) poetic genre of bards. During the same period, under the pen-name of Khatāī ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language "Arabic language"): خطائی for *sinner*) Shah Ismail I wrote about 1,400 verses in Azerbaijani,[\[340\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-minorsky1-343) which were later published as his *Divan*. A unique literary style known as *qoshma* ([Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language "Azerbaijani language"): *qoşma* for *improvisation*) was introduced in this period and developed by Shah Ismail and later by his son and successor, Shah [Tahmasp I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahmasp_I "Tahmasp I").[\[341\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Mino-344)

In the span of the 17th and 18th centuries, Fuzuli's unique genres as well ashik poetry were taken up by prominent poets and writers such as [Qovsi of Tabriz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qovsi_of_Tabriz "Qovsi of Tabriz"), [Shah Abbas Sani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbas_II_of_Persia "Abbas II of Persia"), [Agha Mesih Shirvani](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Agha_Mesih_Shirvani\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Agha Mesih Shirvani (page does not exist)") \[[ru](https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%90%D0%B3%D0%B0_%D0%9C%D0%B0%D1%81%D0%B8%D1%85_%D0%A8%D0%B8%D1%80%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B8 "ru:Ага Масих Ширвани")], Nishat, [Molla Vali Vidadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molla_Vali_Vidadi "Molla Vali Vidadi"), [Molla Panah Vagif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molla_Panah_Vagif "Molla Panah Vagif"), Amani, Zafar and others. Along with Turks, [Turkmens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkmens "Turkmens") and [Uzbeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uzbeks "Uzbeks"), Azerbaijanis celebrate the [Epic of Koroglu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_of_Koroglu "Epic of Koroglu") (from [Azerbaijani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_language "Azerbaijani language"): *kor oğlu* for *blind man's son*), a legendary folk hero.[\[342\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-samuel-345) Several documented versions of Koroglu epic remain at the Institute for Manuscripts of the [National Academy of Sciences of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Academy_of_Sciences_of_Azerbaijan "National Academy of Sciences of Azerbaijan").[\[328\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-caferoglu-331)

### Media

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=32 "Edit section: Media")]

Main articles: [Media of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Azerbaijan "Media of Azerbaijan") and [Media freedom in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_freedom_in_Azerbaijan "Media freedom in Azerbaijan")

The first newspaper in Azerbaijani, *[Akinchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akinchi "Akinchi")* was published in 1875.[\[343\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-346) There are three state-owned television channels: [AzTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AzTV "AzTV"), [Idman TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idman_Azerbaijan_TV "Idman Azerbaijan TV") and [Medeniyyet TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medeniyyet_TV "Medeniyyet TV"). There is one public channel and 6 private channels: [İctimai Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0ctimai_Television "İctimai Television"), Space TV, [Lider TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lider_TV "Lider TV"), [Azad Azerbaijan TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azad_Azerbaijan_TV "Azad Azerbaijan TV"), [Xazar TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xazar_TV "Xazar TV"), [Real TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Real_TV_\(Azerbaijan\)\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Real TV (Azerbaijan) (page does not exist)") \[[az](https://az.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_TV_\(Az%C9%99rbaycan\) "az:Real TV (Azərbaycan)")] and [ARB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Region_TV "Region TV").[\[344\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-347)

### Cinema

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=33 "Edit section: Cinema")]

Main articles: [Cinema of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Azerbaijan "Cinema of Azerbaijan") and [Television in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Azerbaijan "Television in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Film_of_Azerbaijan_1916.jpg/220px-Film_of_Azerbaijan_1916.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Film_of_Azerbaijan_1916.jpg)

Scene from the Azerbaijani film *[In the Kingdom of Oil and Millions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In_the_Kingdom_of_Oil_and_Millions "In the Kingdom of Oil and Millions")*, 1916

The film industry in Azerbaijan dates back to 1898. Azerbaijan was among the first countries involved in cinematography,[\[345\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-mishon-348) with the apparatus first showing up in Baku.[\[346\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cinemainazerbaijan-349) In 1919 a documentary *The Celebration of the Anniversary of Azerbaijani Independence* was filmed on the first anniversary of Azerbaijan's independence from Russia, 27 May, and premiered in June 1919 at several theatres in Baku.[\[347\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ocaz-350) After the Soviet power was established in 1920, [Nariman Narimanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nariman_Narimanov "Nariman Narimanov"), chairman of the Revolutionary Committee of Azerbaijan, signed a decree nationalizing Azerbaijan's cinema. This also influenced the creation of [Azerbaijani animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_animation "Azerbaijani animation").[\[347\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-ocaz-350)

In 1991, after Azerbaijan gained its independence from the Soviet Union, the first Baku International Film Festival East-West was held in Baku. In December 2000, former President Heydar Aliyev signed a decree proclaiming 2 August to be the professional holiday of filmmakers of Azerbaijan. Today Azerbaijani filmmakers are again dealing with issues similar to those faced by cinematographers prior to the establishment of the Soviet Union in 1920. Once again, both choices of content and sponsorship of films are largely left up to the initiative of the filmmaker.[\[345\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-mishon-348)

### Cuisine

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=34 "Edit section: Cuisine")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Az%C9%99rbaycan_dolmas%C4%B1.jpg/240px-Az%C9%99rbaycan_dolmas%C4%B1.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Az%C9%99rbaycan_dolmas%C4%B1.jpg)

[Dolma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolma "Dolma"), a traditional Azerbaijani meal

[Azerbaijani cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_cuisine "Azerbaijani cuisine") uses an abundance of seasonal vegetables and greens. Fresh herbs, including mint, cilantro (coriander), dill, basil, parsley, tarragon, leeks, chives, thyme, marjoram, green onion, and watercress, are popular and often accompany main dishes on the table. Climatic diversity and fertility of the land are reflected in the national dishes, which are based on fish from the Caspian Sea, local meat (mainly mutton and beef), and seasonal vegetables and greens.

Saffron-rice [plov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plov "Plov") is the flagship food in Azerbaijan, and [black tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_tea "Black tea") is the national beverage.[\[348\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-351) Azerbaijanis often use traditional [armudu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armudu "Armudu") (pear-shaped) glass as they have very strong [tea culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijani_tea_culture "Azerbaijani tea culture").[\[349\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-aze-352)[\[350\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-353) Popular traditional dishes include [bozbash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bozbash "Bozbash") (lamb soup that exists in several regional varieties with the addition of different vegetables), [qutab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qutab "Qutab") (fried turnover with a filling of greens or minced meat) and [dushbara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dushbara "Dushbara") (dumplings filled with ground meat and spices).

### Sport

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=35 "Edit section: Sport")]

Main article: [Sport in Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Azerbaijan "Sport in Azerbaijan")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Mamedyarov%2C_Shamkir_2016.JPG/168px-Mamedyarov%2C_Shamkir_2016.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mamedyarov,_Shamkir_2016.JPG)

[Shakhriyar Mamedyarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakhriyar_Mamedyarov "Shakhriyar Mamedyarov") was the 2013 [World Rapid Chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Rapid_Chess_Championship "World Rapid Chess Championship") and three-time [European Team Chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Team_Chess_Championship "European Team Chess Championship") champion

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Teymur_R%C9%99c%C9%99bov.jpg/168px-Teymur_R%C9%99c%C9%99bov.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Teymur_R%C9%99c%C9%99bov.jpg)

[Teimour Radjabov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teimour_Radjabov "Teimour Radjabov") was the 2019 [World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chess_World_Cup_2019 "Chess World Cup 2019") and three-time European Team Chess champion

Freestyle wrestling has been traditionally regarded as Azerbaijan's [national sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_sport "National sport"), in which Azerbaijan has won [fourteen medals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_at_the_Olympics "Azerbaijan at the Olympics"), including four golds, since joining the [International Olympic Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Olympic_Committee "International Olympic Committee"). The most popular sports are [football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_football "Association football") and wrestling.[\[351\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-354)

The [Association of Football Federations of Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Football_Federations_of_Azerbaijan "Association of Football Federations of Azerbaijan"), with 9,122 registered players, is the largest sporting association in the country.[\[352\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-355)[\[353\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-356) The [national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_national_football_team "Azerbaijan national football team") demonstrates relatively low performance in the international arena compared to the nation football clubs. The most successful clubs are [Neftçi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neft%C3%A7i_PFK "Neftçi PFK"), [Qarabağ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qaraba%C4%9F_FK "Qarabağ FK"), and [Gabala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabala_FK "Gabala FK"). In [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_UEFA_Europa_League "2012–13 UEFA Europa League"), Neftchi Baku became the first Azerbaijani team to advance to the group stage of a European competition.[\[354\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-357)[\[355\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-358) In [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_UEFA_Europa_League "2014–15 UEFA Europa League"), Qarabağ became the second Azerbaijani club advancing to the group stage of [UEFA Europa League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Europa_League "UEFA Europa League"). In 2017, after beating [Copenhagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F.C._Copenhagen "F.C. Copenhagen") 2–2 ([a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Away_goals_rule "Away goals rule")) in the play-off round of the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_UEFA_Champions_League "2017–18 UEFA Champions League"), Qarabağ became the first Azerbaijani club to reach the group stage.[\[356\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-359)

[Futsal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futsal "Futsal") is another popular sport in Azerbaijan. The [Azerbaijan national futsal team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_national_futsal_team "Azerbaijan national futsal team") reached fourth place in the [2010 UEFA Futsal Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_UEFA_Futsal_Championship "2010 UEFA Futsal Championship"), while domestic club [Araz Naxçivan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Araz_Nax%C3%A7ivan "Araz Naxçivan") clinched bronze medals at the [2009–10 UEFA Futsal Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_UEFA_Futsal_Cup "2009–10 UEFA Futsal Cup") and [2013–14 UEFA Futsal Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_UEFA_Futsal_Cup "2013–14 UEFA Futsal Cup").[\[357\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-360) Azerbaijan was the main sponsor of Spanish football club [Atlético de Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_de_Madrid "Atlético de Madrid") during seasons 2013/2014 and 2014/2015, a partnership that the club described should 'promote the image of Azerbaijan in the world'.[\[358\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-361)

Azerbaijan is one of the traditional powerhouses of world [chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chess "Chess"),[\[359\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-362) having hosted many international chess tournaments and competitions and became [European Team Chess Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Team_Chess_Championship "European Team Chess Championship") winners in 2009, 2013 and 2017.[\[360\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-363)[\[361\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-364)[\[362\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-365) Notable chess players include [Teimour Radjabov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teimour_Radjabov "Teimour Radjabov"), [Shahriyar Mammadyarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahriyar_Mammadyarov "Shahriyar Mammadyarov"), [Vladimir Makogonov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Makogonov "Vladimir Makogonov"), [Vugar Gashimov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vugar_Gashimov "Vugar Gashimov") and former [World Chess Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Chess_Champion "World Chess Champion") [Garry Kasparov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garry_Kasparov "Garry Kasparov"). As of 2014[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit), country's home of [Shamkir Chess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shamkir_Chess "Shamkir Chess") a category 22 event and one of the highest rated tournaments of all time.[\[363\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-366) [Backgammon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backgammon "Backgammon") also plays a major role in Azerbaijani culture.[\[364\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-367) The game is very popular in Azerbaijan and is widely played among the local public.[\[365\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-368) There are also different variations of backgammon developed and analyzed by Azerbaijani experts.[\[366\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-369)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Closing_ceremony_of_the_IV_Islamic_Solidarity_Games_15.jpg/240px-Closing_ceremony_of_the_IV_Islamic_Solidarity_Games_15.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Closing_ceremony_of_the_IV_Islamic_Solidarity_Games_15.jpg)

[Baku National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_National_Stadium "Baku National Stadium") was used for the first [European Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Games "European Games") in June 2015

[Azerbaijan Women's Volleyball Super League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_Women%27s_Volleyball_Super_League "Azerbaijan Women's Volleyball Super League") placed fourth at the [2005 European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_Women%27s_European_Volleyball_Championship "2005 Women's European Volleyball Championship").[\[367\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-370) Over the last years, clubs like [Rabita Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabita_Baku "Rabita Baku") and [Azerrail Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerrail_Baku "Azerrail Baku") achieved great success at European cups.[\[368\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-371) Azerbaijani volleyball players include likes of [Valeriya Korotenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valeriya_Korotenko "Valeriya Korotenko"), [Oksana Parkhomenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oksana_Parkhomenko "Oksana Parkhomenko"), [Inessa Korkmaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inessa_Korkmaz "Inessa Korkmaz"), [Natalya Mammadova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natalya_Mammadova "Natalya Mammadova"), and [Alla Hasanova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alla_Hasanova "Alla Hasanova").

Other Azerbaijani athletes are [Namig Abdullayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namig_Abdullayev "Namig Abdullayev"), [Toghrul Asgarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toghrul_Asgarov "Toghrul Asgarov"), [Rovshan Bayramov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rovshan_Bayramov "Rovshan Bayramov"), [Sharif Sharifov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharif_Sharifov "Sharif Sharifov"), [Mariya Stadnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariya_Stadnik "Mariya Stadnik") and [Farid Mansurov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farid_Mansurov "Farid Mansurov") in [wrestling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amateur_wrestling "Amateur wrestling"), [Nazim Huseynov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazim_Huseynov "Nazim Huseynov"), [Elnur Mammadli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elnur_Mammadli "Elnur Mammadli"), [Elkhan Mammadov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkhan_Mammadov_\(judoka\) "Elkhan Mammadov (judoka)") and [Rustam Orujov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rustam_Orujov "Rustam Orujov") in judo, [Rafael Aghayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Aghayev "Rafael Aghayev") in karate, [Magomedrasul Majidov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magomedrasul_Majidov "Magomedrasul Majidov") and [Aghasi Mammadov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aghasi_Mammadov "Aghasi Mammadov") in [boxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxing "Boxing"), [Nizami Pashayev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizami_Pashayev "Nizami Pashayev") in [Olympic weightlifting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_weightlifting "Olympic weightlifting"), [Azad Asgarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azad_Asgarov "Azad Asgarov") in [pankration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pankration "Pankration"), [Eduard Mammadov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard_Mammadov "Eduard Mammadov") in kickboxing, and [K-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K-1 "K-1") fighter [Zabit Samedov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zabit_Samedov "Zabit Samedov").

Azerbaijan has a [Formula One racetrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_City_Circuit "Baku City Circuit"), constructed in 2012,[\[369\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-372) and the country hosted its first [Formula One Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_European_Grand_Prix "2016 European Grand Prix") in 2016[\[370\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-373) and the [Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix "Azerbaijan Grand Prix") since 2017. Other annual sporting events held in the country are the [Baku Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku_Cup "Baku Cup") tennis tournament and the [Tour d'Azerbaïdjan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_d%27Azerba%C3%AFdjan "Tour d'Azerbaïdjan") cycling race.

Azerbaijan hosted several major sport competitions since the late 2000s, including the [2013 F1 Powerboat World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_F1_Powerboat_World_Championship_season "2013 F1 Powerboat World Championship season"), [2012 FIFA U-17 Women's World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_FIFA_U-17_Women%27s_World_Cup "2012 FIFA U-17 Women's World Cup"), [2011 AIBA World Boxing Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_AIBA_World_Boxing_Championships "2011 AIBA World Boxing Championships"), [2010 European Wrestling Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_European_Wrestling_Championships "2010 European Wrestling Championships"), [2009 Rhythmic Gymnastics European Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Rhythmic_Gymnastics_European_Championships "2009 Rhythmic Gymnastics European Championships"), [2014 European Taekwondo Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Taekwondo_Championships "European Taekwondo Championships"), [2014 Rhythmic Gymnastics European Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Rhythmic_Gymnastics_European_Championships "2014 Rhythmic Gymnastics European Championships"), and [2016 World Chess Olympiad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/42nd_Chess_Olympiad "42nd Chess Olympiad").[\[371\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-374) Baku was selected to host the [2015 European Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_European_Games "2015 European Games").[\[372\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-375) Baku hosted the fourth [Islamic Solidarity Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Solidarity_Games "Islamic Solidarity Games") in 2017[\[373\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-376) and the [2019 European Youth Summer Olympic Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_European_Youth_Summer_Olympic_Festival "2019 European Youth Summer Olympic Festival"),[\[374\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-377) and was a host of [UEFA Euro 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2020 "UEFA Euro 2020").[\[375\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-378)

4. ^ [***a***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-LaPorte2016_4-0) [***b***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-LaPorte2016_4-1) [***c***](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-LaPorte2016_4-2) LaPorte, Jody (2016). "Semi-presidentialism in Azerbaijan". In [Elgie, Robert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Elgie_\(academic\) "Robert Elgie (academic)"); [Moestrup, Sophia](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Sophia_Moestrup\&action=edit\&redlink=1 "Sophia Moestrup (page does not exist)") (eds.). *Semi-Presidentialism in the Caucasus and Central Asia*. London: Palgrave Macmillan (published 15 May 2016). pp. 91–117. [doi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doi_\(identifier\) "Doi (identifier)"):[10.1057/978-1-137-38781-3\_4](https://doi.org/10.1057%2F978-1-137-38781-3_4). [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\) "ISBN (identifier)") [978-1-137-38780-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-1-137-38780-6 "Special:BookSources/978-1-137-38780-6"). [LCCN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LCCN_\(identifier\) "LCCN (identifier)") [2016939393](https://lccn.loc.gov/2016939393). [OCLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OCLC_\(identifier\) "OCLC (identifier)") [6039791976](https://search.worldcat.org/oclc/6039791976). "LaPorte examines the dynamics of semi-presidentialism in Azerbaijan. Azerbaijan's regime is a curious hybrid, in which semi-presidential institutions operate in the larger context of authoritarianism. The author compares formal Constitutional provisions with the practice of politics in the country, suggesting that formal and informal sources of authority come together to enhance the effective powers of the presidency. In addition to the considerable formal powers laid out in the Constitution, Azerbaijan's president also benefits from the support of the ruling party and informal family and patronage networks. LaPorte concludes by discussing the theoretical implications of this symbiosis between formal and informal institutions in Azerbaijan's semi-presidential regime."

10. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-12)** While often politically aligned with Europe, Azerbaijan is generally considered to be at least mostly in Southwest Asia geographically with its northern part bisected by the [standard Asia–Europe divide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boundaries_between_the_continents_of_Earth "Boundaries between the continents of Earth"), the [Greater Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Caucasus "Greater Caucasus"). The United Nations [classification of world regions](http://unstats.un.org/unsd/methods/m49/m49regin.htm) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20100417070721/http://unstats.un.org/unsd/methods/m49/m49regin.htm) 17 April 2010 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine "Wayback Machine") places Azerbaijan in Western Asia; the [CIA World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA_World_Factbook "CIA World Factbook") places it mostly in Southwest Asia [\[1\]](https://www.cia.gov/the-world-factbook/countries/azerbaijan/) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20210127171042/https://www.cia.gov/the-world-factbook/countries/azerbaijan/) 27 January 2021 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine "Wayback Machine") and [*Merriam-Webster's Collegiate Dictionary*](http://www.m-w.com/dictionary/Azerbaijan) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20230409223406/https://www.merriam-webster.com/dictionary/Azerbaijan) 9 April 2023 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine "Wayback Machine") places it in both; [NationalGeographic.com](https://www.nationalgeographic.org/society/education-resources/?xpop=1) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20231124171218/https://www.nationalgeographic.org/society/education-resources/?xpop=1) 24 November 2023 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine "Wayback Machine"), and [*Encyclopædia Britannica*](https://www.britannica.com/EBchecked/topic/46781/Azerbaijan) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20150529085328/http://www.britannica.com/EBchecked/topic/46781/Azerbaijan) 29 May 2015 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine "Wayback Machine") also place Georgia in Asia. Conversely, some sources place Azerbaijan in Europe such as [Worldatlas.com](http://worldatlas.com/webimage/countrys/eu.htm) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20100815094910/http://www.worldatlas.com/webimage/countrys/eu.htm) 15 August 2010 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine "Wayback Machine").

Unofficial Islamic groups sought to use aspects of Islam to mobilize the population and establish the foundations for a future political struggle.... Unlike Turkey, Azerbaijan does not have the powerful ideological legacy of secularism... the conflict with Armenia has bred frustration that is increasingly being answered by a combined Islamic and nationalist sentiment, especially among younger people... Returning to Tehran in the spring of 1795, he assembled a force of some 60,000 cavalries and infantry and in Shawwal Dhul-Qa'da/May, set off for Azarbaijan, intending to conquer the country between the rivers Aras and Kura, formerly under Safavid control. This region comprised a number of khanates of which the most important was *Qarabagh*, with its capital at Shusha; Ganja, with its capital of the same name; Shirvan across the Kura, with its capital at Shamakhi; and to the north-west, on both banks of the Kura, Christian Georgia (Gurjistan), with its capital at Tiflis."

337. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-Meeker_340-0)** Michael E. Meeker, "The Dede Korkut Ethic", International Journal of Middle East Studies, Vol. 24, No. 3 (Aug. 1992), 395–417. excerpt: The Book of Dede Korkut is an early record of oral Turkic folktales in Anatolia, and as such, one of the mythic charters of Turkish nationalist ideology. The oldest versions of the Book of Dede Korkut consist of two manuscripts copied in the 16th century. The twelve stories that are recorded in these manuscripts are believed to be derived from a cycle of stories and songs circulating among Turkic peoples living in northeastern Anatolia and northwestern Azerbaijan. According to Lewis (1974), an older substratum of these oral traditions dates to conflicts between the ancient Oghuz and their Turkish rivals in Central Asia (the Pecheneks and the Kipchaks), but this substratum has been clothed in references to the 14th-century campaigns of the Akkoyunlu Confederation of Turkic tribes against the Georgians, the Abkhaz, and the Greeks in Trebizond. Such stories and songs would have emerged no earlier than the beginning of the 13th century, and the written versions that have reached us would have been composed no later than the beginning of the 15th century. By this time, the Turkic peoples in question had been in touch with Islamic civilization for several centuries, had come to call themselves "Turcoman" rather than "Oghuz," had close associations with sedentary and urbanized societies, and were participating in Islamized regimes that included nomads, farmers, and townsmen. Some had abandoned their nomadic way of life altogether.
338. **[^](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_ref-Kafadar_341-0)** Cemal Kafadar(1995), "in Between Two Worlds: Construction of the Ottoman states", University of California Press, 1995. Excerpt: "It was not earlier than the fifteenth century. Based on the fact that the author is buttering up both the Akkoyunlu and Ottoman rulers, it has been suggested that the composition belongs to someone living in the undefined border region lands between the two states during the reign of Uzun Hassan (1466–78). G. Lewis, on the other hand, dates the composition "fairly early in the 15th century at least."

## Further reading

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Azerbaijan\&action=edit\&section=39 "Edit section: Further reading")]

- Altstadt, Audrey. *Frustrated Democracy in Post-Soviet Azerbaijan* (2018)
- Broers, Broers Laurence. *Armenia and Azerbaijan: Anatomy of a rivalry* (Edinburgh University Press, 2019).
- Cornell, Svante E. *Azerbaijan since independence* (Routledge, 2015).
- Dragadze, Tamara. "Islam in Azerbaijan: The Position of Women" in *Muslim Women's Choices* (Routledge, 2020) pp. 152–163.
- Elliott, Mark. *Azerbaijan with Georgia* (Trailblazers Publications, 1999).
- Ergun, Ayça. "Citizenship, National Identity, and Nation-Building in Azerbaijan: Between the Legacy of the Past and the Spirit of Independence." *Nationalities Papers* (2021): 1–18. [online](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/B42D38E5F6DA18CDD6FF2A030A851B0D/S0090599220000811a.pdf/citizenship_national_identity_and_nationbuilding_in_azerbaijan_between_the_legacy_of_the_past_and_the_spirit_of_independence.pdf)
- [Goltz, Thomas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Goltz "Thomas Goltz"). *Azerbaijan Diary : A Rogue Reporter's Adventures in an Oil-Rich, War-Torn, Post-Soviet Republic*. M E Sharpe (1998). [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\) "ISBN (identifier)") [978-0-7656-0244-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-7656-0244-2 "Special:BookSources/978-0-7656-0244-2")
- Habibov, Nazim, Betty Jo Barrett, and Elena Chernyak. "Understanding women's empowerment and its determinants in post-communist countries: Results of Azerbaijan national survey." *Women's Studies International Forum.* Vol. 62. Pergamon, 2017.
- Olukbasi, Suha. *Azerbaijan: A Political History*. I.B. Tauris (2011). Focus on post-Soviet era.
